# Design system — Material 3 Expressive (complete spec)

Pingogram follows the **Material 3 Expressive** guidance in full: the color system, type
scale, shape scale, motion system, component set, layout rules, state system and accessibility
rules. This document is normative. If a Material 3 Expressive token or component exists for a
job, use it; never invent a parallel one. When something here conflicts with the current
material.io guidance or the Compose Material 3 API, follow Material and update this file.

Sources of truth (check them, they change):
- https://m3.material.io — Foundations (Color, Typography, Shape, Motion, Layout,
  Accessibility, Interaction states) and every Component page, "Expressive" variants.
- Compose Material 3 release notes + API reference (`androidx.compose.material3`, ≥ 1.4.0).
- Google Fonts page for Google Sans Flex (axis ranges, OFL license).

Every screen is audited against §12 before it is marked done. Rules the code does not follow yet are tracked in
`docs/audit/m3-expressive-gap-analysis.md` (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-19 are written into this file).

---

## 1. Theme root

```kotlin
@Composable
fun PingogramTheme(
    themeMode: ThemeMode = ThemeMode.System,     // System | Light | Dark
    dynamicColor: Boolean = true,                // Android 12+ only
    contrast: Contrast = Contrast.Standard,      // Standard | Medium | High (follows system on 14+)
    amoled: Boolean = false,                     // true-black surfaces in dark
    content: @Composable () -> Unit,
) {
    val dark = themeMode.isDark()
    val colorScheme = rememberPingogramColorScheme(dark, dynamicColor, contrast, amoled)
    val pingogramColors = remember(colorScheme, dark) { pingogramColors(colorScheme, dark) }

    MaterialExpressiveTheme(
        colorScheme = colorScheme,
        motionScheme = MotionScheme.expressive(),
        shapes = PingogramShapes,                 // §4
        typography = pingogramTypography(),       // Google Sans Flex, §3
    ) {
        CompositionLocalProvider(
            LocalPingogramColors provides pingogramColors,          // §2.2, MaterialTheme.pingogramColors
            LocalPingogramSpacing provides DefaultSpacing,          // §7, MaterialTheme.spacing
            LocalRippleThemeConfiguration provides RippleDefaults.InsetFocusRingThemeConfiguration, // §2.3
            content = content,
        )
    }
}
```

- The Expressive API (`MotionScheme.expressive()`, `MaterialShapes`, the `*Emphasized` styles,
  `Shapes.largeIncreased`…) is public from material3 1.5; 1.4.0 keeps it internal. In 1.5.0-beta01
  almost all of it is stable. Only these still need `@OptIn(ExperimentalMaterial3ExpressiveApi::class)`:
  - `MaterialShapes`
  - `RoundedPolygon.toShape()` / `toPath()` and `Morph.toPath()`
  - `LoadingIndicator`, `ContainedLoadingIndicator`, `LoadingIndicatorDefaults`
  - `PullToRefreshDefaults.LoadingIndicator`

  `@ExperimentalMaterial3Api` remains on the bottom sheets, the tooltips and `AppBarWithSearch`. Opt in only where
  an API still requires it (`docs/audit/m3-expressive-gap-analysis.md` §7).

- Root is always `MaterialExpressiveTheme`. `MaterialTheme` (non-expressive) is forbidden.
- `MainActivity` passes the Appearance settings (FR-S2) to `PingogramTheme`. When one changes, every
  color role cross-fades to the new scheme on `defaultEffectsSpec()` (§5, `animateColorScheme`); the
  first scheme shows at once.
- Everything reads tokens via `MaterialTheme.colorScheme`, `MaterialTheme.typography`,
  `MaterialTheme.shapes`, `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`. **No literal `Color(0xFF…)`, `sp`
  font sizes, `dp` radii or `tween()` in feature modules.** Lint rule `PingogramHardcodedToken`
  (custom detekt rule in `build-logic`) fails the build on these.

## 2. Color system

### 2.1 Scheme generation
- Color themes (`docs/adr/0016-color-themes.md`): the user picks the wallpaper's colors or one of 13 generated themes
  (`ThemePalette`) in Settings → Appearance → Theme. `tools/theme/generate.mjs` generates each theme's 6 schemes
  (light + dark × standard/medium/high contrast) with material-color-utilities, the Material Theme Builder algorithm
  (contrast 0 / 0.5 / 1), into `core/ui/.../theme/Color.kt` (`themeColorScheme`), and its splash screens into `:app`.
  Change a seed there and run `npm ci && npm run generate`; never hand-edit the output (CI generates it again and fails
  on a difference).
  - Pingogram, the default: `#2156C4`, the blue of the app icon (`art/`; chosen by the owner 2026-10-19), with
    `SchemeVibrant`, chosen by the owner on 2026-10-19. `SchemeTonalSpot` turned the icon's vivid blue into a
    greyish `#495D92`; Expressive turns `primary` green and Fidelity makes the containers saturated
    (`docs/audit/images/brand-scheme-variants.png`).
  - Basic colors: Blue, Teal, Green, Lime, Yellow, Orange, Red, Pink, Purple, Indigo and Brown, seeded with the Material
    Design 500 colors, with `SchemeTonalSpot` (Material Theme Builder's and Android's default style); Monochrome with
    `SchemeMonochrome`. No style choice: one variant per theme.
  - Every theme keeps text at 4.5:1 or more at standard contrast and raises it with each level (`ThemePalettesTest`);
    dynamic color follows the system.
- Dynamic color (Material You) on Android 12+ by default: `dynamicLightColorScheme` /
  `dynamicDarkColorScheme`. Picking a generated theme turns it off; picking "Wallpaper colors" turns it back on. On Android 14+ those read the
  platform's color roles (`system_primary_light`…); some systems (Samsung's One UI) fill only the
  Android 12 tonal palettes (`system_accent1_*`…) from the wallpaper and leave the roles at AOSP's stock
  blue. So the roles are used only when their primary has the palettes' hue; otherwise the scheme is
  built from the palettes as Compose does on Android 12–13 (`systemDynamicColors`, `core:ui`), and a
  medium or high contrast falls back to the last generated theme picked (Pingogram by default) there.
- Contrast follows the system setting on Android 14+ (`UiModeManager.contrast`), otherwise the
  user's choice in Appearance settings.
- AMOLED: with every theme, in dark mode replace `background`, `surface`, `surfaceContainerLowest` with `#000000`
  and shift the container ladder down one tone; keep all `on*` roles unchanged. Pages that normally sit on a
  container (Settings) turn black too, and their rows move to `surfaceContainer` (§2.2).

### 2.2 Role usage (never pick colors by taste)

| Element | Roles |
|---|---|
| App background / list | `background` / `surface` |
| Cards, sheets, dialogs | `surfaceContainerLow` → `surfaceContainerHighest` ladder by prominence |
| Top app bar (scrolled) | `surfaceContainer` |
| Chat list, Contacts tab and conversation | top bar on `surfaceContainer` (the `Scaffold`'s container); content on a `surface` sheet with `extraLarge` top corners below it (`ContentSheet`, `core:ui`) |
| Selected chat row | `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer`, `large` shape (the expressive list's selected shape), inset `spacing.small` from the sheet's sides and `ListItemDefaults.SegmentedGap` apart |
| Pinned chats' group | rows at rest on `surfaceContainer`, two tones above the chat list's `surface` sheet, so the group shows in dark theme and with AMOLED (the old `surfaceContainerLow` tone, still apart from black); a selected or open row in it as any selected row |
| Panes on expanded windows | each pane its own rounded container (`extraLarge`) on a `surfaceContainer` window, `surface` inside (Settings: the settings page's `surfaceContainerHigh`, rows on `surface`); the empty detail pane shows its placeholder in the same container |
| Settings pages | page and its transparent top bar on `surfaceContainerHigh` (`settingsPageColor()`, `feature:settings`), no `ContentSheet`; rows on `surface`, the segmented list item's own container (`ListItemDefaults.segmentedColors()`): lighter than the page in light, darker in dark, so each row stands out from the page in both themes, and one step above the floating navigation bar's `surfaceContainer`. With AMOLED the page is true black and the rows sit on `surfaceContainer`. Leading icons sit in a tonal circle (`sizes.leadingIconContainer`, 40 dp, Material's leading avatar, so rows keep their heights), one `*Container` / `on*Container` pair per group (Android 16 Settings; `SettingsIconTone`): Settings home's General `primary`, Privacy and security `tertiary`, About `secondary`, `primary` by default on pages; a destructive row's icon `errorContainer`. On a selected row (`secondaryContainer`) the circle is `primary` and its icon `onPrimary`, whatever the group's tone, as avatar placeholders on selected rows. Selected radio row `secondaryContainer` (the list item's selected colors); destructive rows `error` content |
| Profile | as a settings page: the page and its transparent large top bar on `surfaceContainerHigh` (`profilePageColor()`, `feature:profile`; true black with AMOLED), rows on `surface` (`surfaceContainer` with AMOLED), without row icons; "Block user" and "Report" in `error`; the action pills: "Message" `primary` / `onPrimary`, the others `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer`; labels after the status "Premium" `tertiaryContainer`, "Scam" / "Fake" `errorContainer`; the shared media's file and link icons in `secondaryContainer` circles; photos and videos of the grid dimmed in dark theme like the conversation's (§10). Expanded: two panes on `surfaceContainer`, each in its rounded container on the page's color |
| Navigation bar | `surfaceContainer` (the floating bar's toolbar); active indicator `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer` |
| Navigation rail | `surface` (Material's default container); active indicator `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer` |
| Primary actions (FAB, send, CTA) | `primaryContainer` / `onPrimaryContainer` (FAB), `primary` / `onPrimary` (filled button) |
| Jump to bottom | `FloatingActionButton` in `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer` (Material's secondary container FAB style), not the primary action |
| Outgoing bubble | `primaryContainer` / `onPrimaryContainer` |
| Incoming bubble | `surfaceContainerHigh` / `onSurface` |
| Selected bubble | `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer` (`pingogramColors.selectedBubble`); its check at the row's start a filled check circle in `primary`, unselected rings in `outline` |
| Emoji-only message | no container; time and state `onSurfaceVariant`, read checks `primary` |
| Sponsored message | filled `Card` with its default colors (`CardDefaults.cardColors()`: `surfaceContainerHighest` / `onSurface`, a step above incoming bubbles); label "Sponsored" / "Recommended" `onSurfaceVariant`; the advertiser's button `FilledTonalButton` (`secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer`); TDLib's accent color id is not used |
| Reaction badge | `surfaceContainerHighest` / `onSurfaceVariant`, chosen `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer`, a `surface` ring that turns `primary` on the user's own reaction (blended with the container in the draw phase, the same width), so it shows by more than its color (`REQUIREMENTS.md` §6.3; owner, 2026-10-23); a 48 dp touch target around the 28 dp badge. A custom emoji shows its picture at the emoji's size (animated like in texts, single-color ones in the content color); until it does, a dot of the content color as a 38% tint of the badge, never a dark mark |
| Code and hidden spoilers in a bubble | code and `pre` blocks on the bubble's block color; a hidden spoiler a block of `pingogramColors` `spoiler`: the bubble's secondary content as the lightest tint of its container that keeps 3:1 against it (§9), its text transparent |
| Typing in the conversation header | the status in `primary`, after three dots in `primary` |
| Service / date pill | `surfaceContainerHighest` / `onSurfaceVariant` |
| Unread badge | `primary` / `onPrimary`; muted: `surfaceVariant` / `onSurfaceVariant` |
| Mentions / links | `primary`; visited/secondary links `tertiary` |
| Error / failed send / delete | `error`, `errorContainer` / `onErrorContainer`; a failed message shows "Not sent · Tap to retry" in `onErrorContainer` on an `errorContainer` label under its bubble, with the error icon |
| Online indicator | `LocalPingogramColors.online` — Material "harmonized" green (`MaterialColors.harmonize`) toward `primary` |
| Avatar placeholders | in the avatar's shape (§4: circles, a forum's rounded square); 4 pairs from container roles: `primaryContainer`, `secondaryContainer`, `tertiaryContainer`, `surfaceContainerHighest` / `onSurfaceVariant`, with matching `on*`, chosen by `id % 4`. Never the `*Fixed` roles: they keep one light tone in dark theme, and Material says not to use them where contrast matters. On a selected or open row (`secondaryContainer`), placeholders use `primary` / `onPrimary` |
| Secret chat lock | `primary` before the name (chat list rows, search, the conversation's title), as an accent like mentions, not a warning; the state of a chat that waits or ended in the row's `onSurfaceVariant` preview |
| Encryption key image | the *Fixed* tones `primaryFixed`, `tertiaryFixedDim`, `onPrimaryFixed`, `onTertiaryFixedVariant` for its four pixel values, the one place they are used: the image must look the same in both themes, as the other person compares it with theirs; it sits in a `primaryContainer` frame with an `outline` border, and its hexadecimal text is the accessible equivalent |
| Forum topic icons | Telegram's six topic colors mapped to the nearest tonal pair, never drawn themselves: blue `primaryContainer`, green and yellow `secondaryContainer`, violet, rose and red `tertiaryContainer` (with their `on*` roles); the General topic `surfaceContainerHighest` / `onSurfaceVariant` |
| Dividers, outlines | `outlineVariant`; focus rings `outline` |
| Scrims | `scrim` at 32% |
| Disabled | content 38% `onSurface`, container 12% `onSurface` |

- Any extra semantic color (voice waveform, poll bars, reaction glow) is derived in
  `pingogramColors()` (`PingogramColors`, read with `MaterialTheme.pingogramColors`) from the
  scheme (tonal shifts, harmonization) — not hard-coded.
- Surfaces use the tonal container ladder; **no shadow elevation** except FAB and floating toolbars
  (level 3, incl. the floating navigation bar), menus and dialogs (level 2/3) per M3 elevation tokens.

### 2.3 State layers
- Hover 8%, focus 10%, pressed 10%, dragged 16% of the content color, drawn as state layers
  (Compose `ripple()`). These are the material3 defaults. For keyboard focus, `PingogramTheme` uses Material 3
  Expressive's inset focus rings (`RippleDefaults.InsetFocusRingThemeConfiguration` in
  `LocalRippleThemeConfiguration`, material3 1.5): clearer on tablets and with a keyboard than the opacity
  focus layer. Per-ripple `RippleAlpha` overrides are deprecated since material3 1.5. No custom pressed
  colors.

## 3. Typography — Google Sans Flex (variable)

### 3.1 Font asset
- **Google Sans Flex**, SIL Open Font License 1.1, downloaded from Google Fonts and bundled as
  `core/ui/src/main/res/font/google_sans_flex.ttf` (variable TTF) with its license shipped as
  `core/ui/src/main/assets/licenses/google_sans_flex_OFL.txt` (`res/font/` accepts only font
  files) and an entry in the OSS licenses screen. Bundling (not Downloadable Fonts) keeps the `foss`
  flavor independent of Play Services and avoids first-launch flashes.
- Verify the axis table of the downloaded file with `fonttools varLib.instancer -h` /
  `ttx -t fvar`. Expected axes (check ranges in the file):

| Axis | Tag | Use in Pingogram |
|---|---|---|
| Weight | `wght` | the Material 3 token weights (400, 500); emphasized styles +100 |
| Width | `wdth` | 100 for every style |
| Optical size | `opsz` | **always set equal to the style's font size in sp** (auto-opsz is not applied by Android) |
| Grade | `GRAD` | 0 in both themes (the bundled file's range is 0–100; Android's SystemUI type scale uses 0); 25 with high contrast (§10) |
| Slant | `slnt` | 0; italic = `slnt −10` (true variable slant, never fake italic) |
| Round | `ROND` | **100 for the emphasized styles** (the rounded look Material 3 Expressive gives emphasis), 0 for the base styles |

- These settings follow Android's own type scale for the font: the SystemUI type scale (AOSP,
  `TypeScaleTokens.kt` in `frameworks/base/packages/SystemUI/compose/core`) and the variable font families of a
  device's font customization (`variable-<style>` and `variable-<style>-emphasized` in `fonts_customization.xml`).
  Both keep the Material 3 sizes and line heights.

- Ranges in the bundled file (read from its `fvar` table): `opsz` 6–144, `wdth` 25–151,
  `wght` 1–1000, `GRAD` **0–100**, `ROND` 0–100, `slnt` −10–0.
- `GRAD` has no negative range, so a dark-theme −25 would be clamped to 0. The owner dropped it on 2026-10-19:
  text renders at grade 0 in both themes, as in Android's SystemUI type scale.
- High contrast (the app's setting, or the system's on Android 14+, which the theme follows) sets every style at
  `GRAD` 25: thicker strokes at the same advance widths, so nothing reflows (`GoogleSansFlexRenderingTest`). Standard
  and medium contrast stay at 0. `pingogramTypography(fontWeightAdjustment, contrast)` caches one typography per
  adjustment and grade, so standard and medium share one, and each grade's fonts are made once per process.
- Small optical sizes set text wider and taller, as the font intends. Compared with the default `opsz` 18,
  `opsz` 14 sets a line about 5% wider and `opsz` 11–12 about 8–9% wider, with an x-height of 0.525–0.530 em
  instead of 0.510. So the same sp reads slightly larger than in apps that leave `opsz` at its default. This is
  kept on purpose. Density is handled by the layout (§7), not by changing the type.

### 3.2 Compose implementation
```kotlin
private fun gsf(weight: Int, opsz: Float, rond: Float, grad: Int = 0, wdth: Float = 100f, slnt: Float = 0f) =
    Font(
        resId = R.font.google_sans_flex,
        weight = FontWeight(weight),
        style = if (slnt != 0f) FontStyle.Italic else FontStyle.Normal,
        variationSettings = FontVariation.Settings(
            FontVariation.weight(weight),
            FontVariation.Setting("opsz", opsz),
            FontVariation.Setting("ROND", rond),
            FontVariation.grade(grad),
            FontVariation.width(wdth),
            FontVariation.slant(slnt),
        ),
    )

// One FontFamily per type style so opsz/ROND/GRAD match that style exactly.
fun gsfFamily(weights: List<Int>, opsz: Float, rond: Float, grad: Int) = FontFamily(
    weights.flatMap { w ->
        listOf(gsf(w, opsz, rond, grad), gsf(w, opsz, rond, grad, slnt = -10f))
    },
)
```
- Every distinct variation setting becomes its own Typeface when first used, so families stay
  small: a regular style holds its weight and its emphasized weight (what a bold span resolves to),
  an emphasized style only its own weight, each upright and italic. No style synthesizes a weight or
  slant (`FontSynthesis.None`): a bold span takes the heaviest font of its style instead of a faked
  bold. The typography is the same in both themes, built once and cached (`core/ui/.../theme/Type.kt`).
- The fonts are `GoogleSansFlexFont`s (`core/ui/.../theme/GoogleSansFlexFont.kt`) rather than
  `Font(R.font.google_sans_flex, …)`: their loader makes each typeface once per process. Compose's
  own typeface caches hold 16 entries, fewer than the fonts a list and a conversation use, and each
  reload made a new typeface whose text the platform laid out and rasterized again.
- `pingogramTypography()` builds the **full M3 Expressive type scale** (display, headline,
  title, body, label × large/medium/small = 15 styles) **and all 15 `*Emphasized` variants**
  from the `Typography` constructor parameters, with `GRAD` 0 (25 with high contrast, §3.1).
- Mapping (weight / opsz / ROND): display, headline, `titleLarge` and body 400 · `titleMedium`,
  `titleSmall` and label 500 (the Material 3 token weights), each with `opsz` = size and ROND 0.
  Emphasized: weight +100 and ROND 100, as the emphasized variable font families of Android's font customization.
  (Compose's emphasized tokens, still marked provisional in material3, put `titleMedium`, `titleSmall` and the
  labels at 700; those families use 600.)
- Sizes and line heights: the M3 token values. Letter spacing: Google's for Google Sans Flex (the
  SystemUI type scale): 0, and 0.1 sp for `bodySmall`, `labelMedium` and `labelSmall`. The M3 tokens'
  tracking (up to 0.5 sp) is Roboto's: the font's optical size axis already spaces small sizes
  apart, and both together set text wider than Material lays it out. `lineHeightStyle`
  = `LineHeightStyle(Alignment.Center, Trim.None)`; `platformStyle = PlatformTextStyle(includeFontPadding = false)`.
- Font scaling: all sizes in `sp`; the chat "message text size" setting multiplies `bodyLarge`
  only (0.85–1.3) and re-derives `opsz`: the theme hands conversations that style as `MaterialTheme.chatStyle`
  (`messageText`, `pingogramMessageText`, `core:ui`), leaving the typography's `bodyLarge` as it is. Non-linear font scaling (Android 14) is respected.
- Bold text (Android 12+): `PingogramTheme` passes `Configuration.fontWeightAdjustment` to
  `pingogramTypography`, which registers each font at its weight plus the adjustment. Compose adds the
  adjustment both when it picks a font from the family and to that font's `wght`, so fonts registered
  at their plain weights would pick the heavier emphasized font and add the adjustment on top; this way
  text gets bolder by the adjustment once (`GoogleSansFlexRenderingTest`).
- Fallbacks: Android's font fallback chain renders glyphs Google Sans Flex lacks (CJK, Arabic,
  Devanagari, emoji) with the system fonts and Noto; no font files are bundled for them.
- Tabular figures for times/counters: `fontFeatureSettings = "tnum"`.
- Material's typography (August 2026) adapts line heights to the script's height. We track it until
  Compose exposes it, and keep the M3 line heights meanwhile.

### 3.3 Style assignment

| Element | Style |
|---|---|
| Large flexible app bar titles (profile) | `displaySmallEmphasized` collapsing to `titleLargeEmphasized`; the status under it `bodyLarge` (`primary` while online), its labels `labelMediumEmphasized` |
| Profile rows, action pills, shared media | values `bodyLarge` over labels in the list item's supporting style; section headers `titleSmallEmphasized` in `primary`; "Message" `labelLargeEmphasized`, the other pills `labelLarge`; tabs as the folder tabs; file and link rows `bodyLarge` / `bodyMedium`; grid labels `labelSmall` |
| Medium flexible app bar titles (Settings pages) | `headlineMediumEmphasized` collapsing to `titleLargeEmphasized` |
| Tab headers (Chats, Contacts, Settings home; §6 "Chat list top bar") | the tab's title ("Chats", "Contacts", "Settings") `displaySmallEmphasized` collapsing to `titleLargeEmphasized` (owner decision 2026-11-07); the status line `bodyMedium`, its unread count in `bodyMediumEmphasized`'s weight |
| Screen titles in a small app bar (archive, search) | `titleLargeEmphasized` |
| Conversation title / status | `titleMediumEmphasized` / `bodySmall` |
| Auth step headline | `headlineMediumEmphasized`, with a `MaterialShapes` icon badge above |
| Chat list row title | `titleMediumEmphasized` when unread, `titleMedium` when read |
| Chat list preview / message text | `bodyMedium` (one line) / `bodyLarge` |
| Timestamps, badges, meta | `labelSmall` (`tnum`) |
| Verification codes | `headlineSmall` (`tnum`), centered, tracked 0.1 em wider (`typography.verificationCode`, `core:ui`); word and phrase codes `titleLarge` as typed text |
| Passcode entry (lock screen, Passcode lock) | title `headlineMediumEmphasized` under a `MaterialShapes` badge, as an auth step; hint and errors `bodyMedium`; keypad digits `headlineSmall` |
| Buttons | filled (primary) buttons `labelLargeEmphasized`; tonal, outlined and text buttons `labelLarge` |
| Section headers in settings | `titleSmallEmphasized` in `primary` (settings pages; Settings home has none, §6) |
| Account card name (Settings home) | `titleLarge`; phone `bodyLarge`, username `bodyMedium` in `onSurfaceVariant`; "Premium" label `labelMediumEmphasized` |
| Empty state title / body | `headlineSmallEmphasized` / `bodyMedium` |
| Code blocks in messages | `bodyMedium` + `FontFamily.Monospace` (system) |
| Sponsored message label / sponsor / text / button | `labelMedium` ("Sponsored", "Recommended") / `titleSmallEmphasized` / `bodyMedium` / `labelLarge` |
| "About this ad" title / report question | `titleLargeEmphasized` / `titleMedium` |

## 4. Shape system

- Shape scale (M3 Expressive): `extraSmall 4` · `small 8` · `medium 12` · `large 16` ·
  `largeIncreased 20` · `extraLarge 28` · `extraLargeIncreased 32` · `extraExtraLarge 48` · full = pill.
- `MaterialShapes` (the 35 expressive polygons) are the only source of non-rounded-rect shapes. They are used for:
  - states: the selected avatar's `SoftBurst` check, the call screen avatar (`Cookie9Sided` turning while the call rings, morphing to `Cookie12Sided` once answered)
  - story rings
  - the QR login frame (`Cookie12Sided`), the auth step badges and the passcode entry's badge (`Gem`, `Cookie4Sided` on
    error)
  - loading indicator polygons
  - empty-state illustrations: soft shapes, so errors use `Cookie4Sided`, never the spiky `Burst`

  Avatars and their placeholders are circles for people, bots, groups, channels and Saved Messages (which keeps its
  icon placeholder): a person's picture reads best in a plain circle, and six silhouettes in one list read as noise.
  Forums (supergroups with topics, td_api.tl `supergroup.is_forum`) take a rounded square: the shape scale's `large`
  corners at the avatar's size, which tells a chat that opens topics apart from a group (owner decision 2026-11-06).
  One rule decides it, `core:ui`'s `AvatarShape.ofChat`, wherever a chat's avatar shows: the chat list, search, the
  forward and share sheets, the conversation's title, the info page's header (its collapse morph starts from it) and
  the home screen widget. The selected avatar's check morphs from that shape.
- **Shape morphing** (`Morph`, `MaterialShapes.*.toShape()`, `animateFloatAsState` with a
  spatial spring): FAB press (`Circle → Cookie9Sided`), toggle buttons (`Circle ↔ Square`),
  selected avatar, record button while recording. A reaction badge added to a bubble scales in instead, without a
  burst (§5.1).
- Content sheets (chat list, conversation): `extraLarge` top corners, square at the bottom edge of
  the window; content scrolls inside them, clipped to the corners.
- List rows in a sheet sit `spacing.small` in from its sides and half of
  `ListItemDefaults.SegmentedGap` from the rows around (their content keeps the list item's
  padding): at rest `ListItem`'s `extraSmall`, pressed, open and selected in selection mode `large` (the expressive
  list's selected shape), morphing on the list item's fast spatial spring. Swipe actions reveal the same inset
  container with `large` corners, and the row being dragged shows its own `large` container (as when pressed) so it
  never slides with a square edge.
- Settings rows are Material 3 Expressive segmented list items (`SegmentedListItem`): each row its own
  container, `ListItemDefaults.SegmentedGap` (2 dp) apart, with the shapes of
  `ListItemDefaults.segmentedShapes(index, count)`: `large` outer corners at the ends of a group
  (top of the first row, bottom of the last, all four for a single row) and `extraSmall` between
  rows; pressed, focused and selected rows morph to `large` all round on the motion scheme's fast
  spatial spring (the list item's own animation). The account card at the top of Settings rests on
  `extraLarge` and morphs to the same `large` when pressed.
- Message bubbles take Material 3 Expressive's rounded shapes: `extraLarge` corners, which
  make a one-line bubble a full pill (corners are capped at half the height), and no tail.
  Consecutive messages of a sender stack `spacing.extraSmall` apart and meet with `extraSmall`
  corners on the sender's side (the end for outgoing, the start for incoming): the first of a group
  is `extraLarge` at the top and `extraSmall` at the bottom there, a middle one `extraSmall` at
  both, the last `extraSmall` at the top and `extraLarge` at the bottom; the far side stays
  `extraLarge`, and a message alone is `extraLarge` all round. The eight shapes are built once per
  theme (`MessageBubbleShapes`, `core:ui`).
- Media in bubbles: photos, videos and albums clip to the same shape, square only where a header or a caption joins
  them. The media viewer's container transform morphs from and back to exactly that clip shape. A photo or video takes
  the bubble's full media width (`sizes.mediaMaxWidth`, the widest a bubble gets on a phone), as tall as its aspect
  ratio makes it within `sizes.mediaMinSize` and `sizes.mediaMaxHeight`, the picture cropped to fill it: a portrait
  photo shows its middle at the full width rather than a narrow picture beside empty room.
- Padding: text and the time keep `spacing.medium` from the sides and `spacing.small` from top and bottom, clear of
  the round corners.
- Time: the time and the state show on the last bubble of a sender's group only (and on every message that stands
  alone), so a run of messages is not crowded with times. Tapping a bubble shows its time, and screen readers always
  hear it.
- Radius: user-adjustable in Settings → Appearance → Chat appearance, one of the shape scale's `medium`, `large`,
  `largeIncreased`, `extraLarge` (the default) or `extraLargeIncreased`; `MaterialTheme.chatStyle.bubbleShapes` are
  the eight shapes built with it in place of `extraLarge`, the joined corners `extraSmall` as ever.
- Reply quotes and link previews are tonal rounded cards (`medium` corners, the bubble's block color) without a
  leading accent bar. A quote reply's card (and the composer's reply bar) shows the quoted part, after a quote mark:
  Material Symbols `format_quote` at `sizes.inlineIconSmall` in the card's content color (the bar's icon in
  `primary`), decorative in bubbles, whose description says "Quoting Ada: …". Inline code, `pre` blocks and hidden spoilers sit on `small`-rounded backgrounds, drawn behind
  the text from its layout (`textBlocks`, `core:ui`; a span background can only be a rectangle): inline code and
  spoilers one per line they cover, a `pre` block one around all of its lines, each `sizes.textBlockPadding` wider than
  its text at both ends. They are computed once per layout and drawn in the draw phase. A hidden spoiler is a plain
  tonal block, without noise or animation. Voice messages keep their waveform.
- Stickers have no container: their artwork is transparent. Until the picture shows, the sticker's emoji stands in;
  a sticker without one shows a `surfaceContainerHigh` placeholder with `extraLarge` corners, never a hard-edged
  block.
- Emoji-only messages (1–3 emoji, regular or custom, and nothing else, no other formatting, reply or forward) show in
  `displaySmall` without a bubble, the time and state below them.
- Custom emoji (`docs/adr/0022-custom-emoji-rendering.md`) take the place of the emoji they stand for, in the text's
  line, without a container: the picture fills the largest square of that emoji's glyph box (its width, as tall as the
  line allows), centred, so a line with custom emoji is as tall as without; in an emoji-only message they are that
  large in `displaySmall`. Until a picture shows, and where none loads, the regular emoji stands in, never a blank or a
  placeholder shape; the picture then replaces it at once (no fade: both show the same emoji). Animated ones play like
  stickers (§5, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` §4); single-color ones (`needs_repainting`) take the text's color (the bubble's
  `on*` content role, `onSurface` without a bubble); none are dimmed in dark theme (artwork, §10). Within a hidden
  spoiler they stay hidden. The composer's field shows them the same way, keeping the cursor visible beside them.
- Reactions sit on a bubble's bottom corner on the far side as round badges (§2.2), up to three
  side by side, then "+N", which opens the message's actions; they overlap the bubble by its bottom
  padding, so they never cover the time, and hang below content without a bubble. Each takes a 48 dp touch
  target without moving the layout.
- Components keep their M3 default shape tokens (buttons pill, cards `medium`, sheets
  `extraLarge` top, dialogs `extraLarge`, chips `small`, text fields `extraSmall` top).

## 5. Motion system

- `MotionScheme.expressive()`; animation specs come **only** from
  `MaterialTheme.motionScheme`:
  - **Spatial** springs (movement, size, shape; slight overshoot): `defaultSpatialSpec()`,
    `fastSpatialSpec()`, `slowSpatialSpec()`.
  - **Effects** springs (color, alpha, blur; no overshoot): `defaultEffectsSpec()`,
    `fastEffectsSpec()`, `slowEffectsSpec()`.
- Mapping, by size as Material has it: screen transitions, sheet open/close, bars, panes, list reordering, shape
  morphs → spatial default; press feedback, FAB menu, chips, small pops → spatial fast; a whole screen growing out
  of a button or a thumbnail → spatial slow on the way in, spatial default on the way back; color/alpha
  crossfades → effects default; reactions, typing dots, send confirmation → effects fast. The owner's decisions of
  2026-11-11 on what the code and this section left open are in §5.1.
- Typing dots (the conversation header, while someone types or does something else in the chat): three dots before
  the text, which says what is happening. Each brightens from 38% to full opacity and fades back on
  `fastEffectsSpec`, the next starting as it fades; the opacity is read in the draw phase, so nothing composes again
  per frame (`TypingDots`, `feature:chat`). Under an `InfiniteAnimationPolicy` (Compose UI tests) the loop runs as the
  policy allows; with reduced motion the dots stand still at full opacity.
- Navigation transitions:
  - **Shared-axis X** for every screen pushed above a tab: conversation, archive, search, settings pages. It is
    built in `core:ui` (`sharedAxisXEnter` / `sharedAxisXExit`): a slide by `SharedAxisDistance` on
    `defaultSpatialSpec` with fades on the effects springs, mirrored on pop. It moves to material3's
    `MaterialTransitions.HorizontalSharedAxis` once 1.6 is published.
  - **Shared-axis Y** for vertical flows (auth).
  - **Fade-through** only for switching the navigation tabs (Chats, Contacts, Settings) and between the signed-out and signed-in graphs (§5.1). Chat folders slide instead (owner decision 2026-11-07, §6 "Folder tabs"), and crossfade on the effects springs with reduced motion.
  - **Container transform** via `SharedTransitionLayout` for avatar → profile (the conversation title's avatar into
    the pushed profile or info page, `avatarTransform`), thumbnail → media viewer, the New chat button → New chat, and the
    search button → search. A whole screen growing out of a button keeps the layout it ends with and is revealed by
    the growing container (`containerTransform`'s `fixedLayout`), so no frame measures its lists and bars anew.
- Predictive back: Navigation's predictive back with our transitions (`predictiveBackPopExit`,
  `core:ui`): the screen being left scales to 90% on `defaultSpatialSpec` and fades on
  `slowEffectsSpec` over the destination beneath, which shows at full opacity. Navigation seeks the
  transition with the gesture's progress and plays the rest after release at the same pace, so the
  springs' curves are the gesture's curve. Navigation's default (scale to 70% without fading)
  leaves an opaque card that is cut at the end. Custom surfaces
  (media viewer, attach sheet, selection toolbar) implement `PredictiveBackHandler` and scale
  to 90% with the Expressive back-progress curve. The login flow, one destination whose steps come from TDLib, seeks
  its shared-axis Y step transition (a `SeekableTransitionState`, `AuthStepTransition`) towards the step back leads
  to, which its view model derives with the same reduction as back itself; a cancelled gesture returns on
  `defaultEffectsSpec`.
- Lists: `Modifier.animateItem()` on every `LazyColumn` item; a new message fades in place while its
  neighbours slide (§5.1); swipe actions reveal icons with scale springs; pull/overscroll uses
  the stretch overscroll default.
- Loading: `LoadingIndicator` (morphing polygons) for indeterminate, `LinearWavyProgressIndicator`
  / `CircularWavyProgressIndicator` for determinate (uploads, downloads, voice playback, the login's flood wait and
  resend countdowns, beside their text and hidden from screen readers). A screen whose
  content usually comes from TDLib's local database at once shows its indicator only after **200 ms** (Material's
  threshold: nothing under 200 ms, a loading indicator up to 5 s, progress beyond), fading in on
  `defaultEffectsSpec` (`DelayedLoadingIndicator`, `core:ui`), so it never flashes. Every such screen uses it:
  chat list, conversation, settings pages, Appearance, the auth progress step.
- Micro-interactions with haptics (`LocalHapticFeedback`):
  - `LongPress`: selection, the message menu, starting a voice recording
  - `TextHandleMove`: swipe threshold (chat rows, swipe to reply, a voice recording's slide to cancel or lock)
  - `Confirm`: sent, a code or password accepted
  - `Reject`: failed send, wrong code or password, a voice recording too short to send (`Confirm` and `Reject` on API
    34+; auth gets them as view model effects once Telegram answers, never from guesses in the UI)
  - `ToggleOn` / `ToggleOff`: switches and reactions
  - `SegmentTick`: slider steps and choices in a connected button group

  No raw `Vibrator` patterns.
- Reduced motion: if animator duration scale is 0, replace springs with snaps and skip shape
  morphs; essential information is never conveyed by motion alone. Compose already ends every animation at once then;
  `isReducedMotion()` (`core:ui`) follows the scale while the app runs (Android 13+: `ValueAnimator`'s duration scale
  listener, which also sees Battery Saver; before: the `ANIMATOR_DURATION_SCALE` setting), and what goes through
  `core:ui` steps aside with it: shape morphs snap to their end (`rememberMorphShape`, the selected avatar,
  `snapWhenReducedMotion`), container transforms turn off.
- Performance rule: animate with `graphicsLayer {}` / `Modifier.offset {}` lambdas, never by
  recomposing layout for each frame (see `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`).

### 5.1 Motion decisions (owner, 2026-11-11)

The owner's answers to the questions of `docs/audit/motion-expressive-audit.md` (Q-01 to Q-26), checked against
Material's rule of speed by size (fast for small components, default for what covers part of the screen, slow for
what fills it) and against what material3's own components do in the pinned versions (the audit's 1.1 and §6). Where
a row of §6 or §13 differs, this subsection wins; the rows are edited, and the code changed, by the pull requests of
`docs/plans/motion-expressive-plan.md`.

- **Speeds**
  - Bars and blocks that grow in (the pinned, translate and scheduled bars, banners, suggestions) shrink away on
    `defaultSpatialSpec` too. A fast exit stays only where §6 names one: the sticker suggestions and the floating
    navigation bar (Q-01).
  - Fades come in on `defaultEffectsSpec` and leave on `fastEffectsSpec`, everywhere (Q-02).
  - A whole screen growing out of a button or a thumbnail (New chat, search, avatar → profile, thumbnail → media
    viewer) moves its bounds and corners on `slowSpatialSpec` on the way in and on `defaultSpatialSpec` on the way
    back, as material3's full-screen search bar expands and collapses. Shared-axis pushes keep `defaultSpatialSpec`:
    they move a screen by `SharedAxisDistance`, not across the window. The media viewer's transform changes only once
    `OpenMediaBenchmark` shows it keeps its frames (Q-03).
  - Panes move on `defaultSpatialSpec`, read from the scheme: their bounds, their enter and exit, and the third
    pane's handle. A pane covers part of the window, and material3-adaptive moves its panes on the same values (Q-04).
  - A collapsing header settles as material3's top bar does, on its scroll behavior's own spec (Q-05).
  - A committed folder swipe and a tab tap both land the lists on `defaultSpatialSpec` (Q-07).
  - The record pill springs back on `fastSpatialSpec` after a release, a cancel or a lock (Q-17).
- **Transitions**
  - Signing in and out fade through (Q-06).
  - New story's two steps move on the shared axis X. The shared media tabs' content crossfades on the effects
    springs (Q-18).
  - A new message fades in place while its neighbours slide (Q-13).
  - A reaction badge added to a bubble scales in on `fastSpatialSpec`, read in its draw phase. No burst (Q-12).
  - In calls, video tiles fade in and out and the voice and video layouts crossfade, on the effects springs; a
    tile's bounds do not animate (Q-15). Floating reactions rise on `slowSpatialSpec` and fade on `slowEffectsSpec`,
    and do not show with reduced motion or while power saving stops the call screen animations (Q-14).
  - The emoji panel and a bot keyboard grow in and shrink away on `defaultSpatialSpec` when no system keyboard is
    involved; switching to and from the keyboard still moves nothing (Q-11).
- **Back**
  - Step-backs inside a screen preview with the back gesture, as the login flow's do: the settings flows, New story,
    Add contact's "not on Telegram" step, a topic or thread in the detail pane, docked search, and the emoji panel
    (Q-08, Q-11).
  - With animations off, the login flow's gesture previews nothing: the step changes when the gesture commits (Q-19).
  - A cancelled back gesture in the media and stories viewers returns on `defaultSpatialSpec`, and a new gesture
    cancels the return (Q-22).
- **Gestures and scrolling**
  - Tapping the selected tab scrolls its list to the top on `defaultSpatialSpec` (a jump to within a screen of the
    top first, when further away). Other programmatic scrolls keep Compose's own (Q-09).
  - Chat rows and sessions keep `SwipeToDismissBox`'s own settle (Q-10). The session swipe's threshold haptic fires
    only when the user's own drag crosses it (Q-25).
  - The message menu waits for the lifted message's return only when the message had been moved to fit (Q-16).
  - Pinch zoom stops at its limits without a rubber band, and the stories viewer closes on a swipe down only (Q-24).
    The fast-scroll index's dot jumps between letters (Q-25). Story segments and the video seek bar keep their 32 ms
    steps (Q-23).
- **Layout**
  - Progress while Telegram answers sits in a slot whose height is always kept, above the buttons, so no button
    moves. The login flow keeps its progress in the button (Q-20).
  - Rows that depend on a switch grow in and shrink away inside their settings group (Q-21).
- **Lint**: the motion rule covers `:app` as it covers the features; `core:ui` is checked for literal tweens,
  durations and easings only (Q-26, `docs/TESTING.md` once the rule lands).

## 6. Component mapping (screen → M3 Expressive components)

| UI element | Component |
|---|---|
| Chat list top bar | Owner decision (2026-11-06): a large header, Material's large flexible top bar built from its parts (`TwoRowsTopAppBar`), built once in `core:ui` as `TabHeader` and shared with the Contacts tab and Settings home (owner decision 2026-11-07), transparent on `surfaceContainer` with the list on a rounded sheet below (`ContentSheet`), collapsing as the list scrolls (`exitUntilCollapsedScrollBehavior`, read in the layout and draw phases) and coming back at its top. **Expanded**: "Chats" in `displaySmallEmphasized`, and under it the status line in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`: the main list's unread unmuted chats in `primary` and the emphasized weight, then "· 3 mentions" (chats with unread mentions), worded with plurals by `ChatListViewModel.summary` and read by the header alone, one text for screen readers, left out while nothing is unread. While not connected the connection pill (FR-L5) takes the status line's place. **Collapsed**: the dense bar's height (56 dp, `sizes.denseTopAppBar`), "Chats" in `titleLargeEmphasized`; the status line goes, and while not connected one line in `bodySmall` `onSurfaceVariant` says the connection's state (owner decision to confirm, 2026-11-06: FR-L5 asks for it in the bar). **Actions** at the end: search, a `FilledTonalIconButton` (search symbol, `PlainTooltip`, `IconButtonDefaults.shapes()`), which grows into search with a container transform (§5) and is away while search shows; then the signed-in user's avatar (`sizes.avatarExtraSmall`) in an `IconButton`, "Your profile" for its tooltip and screen readers, which opens the Settings tab, and whose long press opens the account menu as the Settings tab's long press does (owner decision to confirm, 2026-11-06). While the user hid the archive row, "Archived chats" comes first, the same button with the archive symbol and the archive's unread chats in a `secondary` / `onSecondary` `Badge` (the row's muted `surfaceVariant` would vanish on the button's `secondaryContainer`). In selection mode the title says how many are selected, with close, and no actions or status. The folder tabs stay below the header, collapsed or not (owner decision to confirm, 2026-11-06). The archive keeps a small pinned `TopAppBar` at the dense height, `titleLargeEmphasized`; the archive's one action is "Archive settings" (the settings symbol, the same button), never an overflow menu |
| Global search | **Compact and medium** (one pane): a full-screen destination (`SearchRoute`) pushed above the tab, with the M3 search bar expanded, built from `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` with the bar's colors, divider and full-screen insets; the field, the chips and the divider sit on the bar's container color, opaque, as the results scroll under them. Not `ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar`, which opens a dialog window that would stay above the chats a result opens and outside predictive back. Medium windows keep it: they have one pane, so there is no list beside a detail to dock into, and a docked popup over a single pane would hide what it searches. **Expanded and wider**: docked, as Material asks (owner decision D6): the field and its results take the place of the chat list in the Chats tab's list pane (`SearchPane`), from the Chats tab, and a result opens in the detail pane (a message at that message) while search stays; back closes that conversation first, then search. A window that shrinks while search is docked keeps it in its one pane, full window. Opened from the search button of the Chats tab's header (owner decision 2026-11-06, on every window), which grows into search with a container transform (§5) and is away while search shows, keeping its place. Under the field: a row of `FilterChip`s (`FilterChipDefaults.shapes()`, scrolling sideways where they do not fit): All, Chats (the user's and public chats), Contacts, Messages, Media (photos and videos) and Links (TDLib's `searchMessagesFilterPhotoAndVideo` / `searchMessagesFilterUrl`); Media and Links also find without a query, every one in the chats. Last, Posts (td_api.tl `searchPublicPosts`, posts of public channels): Telegram allows a few free searches a day, so typing only reads its limits (`getPublicPostSearchLimits`) and a card above where the posts show offers the search, on the result rows' container with `large` corners: the search symbol and "Search posts in public channels" in `titleMedium` (a heading), "Each search uses one of your free searches for the day. 7 of 10 are left." in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`, and a default `Button` "Search posts" at its end; the posts found then show as the messages do, under "Posts in public channels", followed by "6 of 10 free searches left today" in `bodySmall` `onSurfaceVariant`, paged as messages are. With none left, the card shows the clock symbol, "You used today's 10 free searches" and when the next free one comes, that Telegram offers more for Telegram Stars, which Pingogram can't pay (a polite live region), and no button: Pingogram never pays Telegram Stars for a search. Owner decision to confirm (2026-11-10): the search runs only on the button, never as the query is typed, so typing spends no free search; the keyboard's search key only hides the keyboard; and a search is not run again by itself after process death A chip whose messages TDLib counted shows about how many, after its label in `labelMedium` with tabular figures ("999+" above 999), and screen readers hear one text ("Messages, 42 results") (owner decision 2026-11-06): Messages from the search All or Messages made, Media and Links from their own, for the query searched, only from searches already made (never a request of its own), none for a chip not searched yet, and none while locked chats are hidden, whose messages TDLib's totals would count. Results come in sections, each a group of segmented list items (`ListItemDefaults.segmentedShapes` / `segmentedColors`, `SegmentedGap` apart, inset by the window margin as settings rows are) with gaps between groups, under `titleSmallEmphasized` labels; a section still searching or that found nothing is left out. One `LinearWavyProgressIndicator` over the divider under the chips while anything searches, after 200 ms (§5), taking no height so nothing moves; a section's error (or the next page's, ending the messages' group) in an `errorContainer` segment: the error icon, the text and a "Try again" `TextButton` in `onErrorContainer` |
| Conversation top bar | `TopAppBar` small, avatar + two-line title (`titleMediumEmphasized`), on `surfaceContainer` with the messages on a rounded sheet below (`ContentSheet`). At most two filled icon-button actions (Material: avoid overflow menus in app bars), `FilledTonalIconButton`s with `IconButtonDefaults.shapes()`. Every chat (and topic and thread) has search ("search", "Search in chat", §6 "In-chat search") first; a private chat with a person then the call button ("call", REQUIREMENTS.md FR-V1), never an overflow menu or buttons that do nothing. Owner decision (2026-10-23): it stays one button; where the other user can get a video call (td_api.tl `userFullInfo.can_be_called` and `supports_video_calls`) and the device has a camera, a tap opens an expressive menu anchored to it (`DropdownMenuPopup`, one `DropdownMenuGroup` with `MenuDefaults.groupShape` and `itemShape`): "Voice call" (`ic_call`) and "Video call" (`ic_videocam`), outlined symbols as menus have them (`CallButtonMenu`, `core:ui`); where only a voice call is possible, a tap calls at once. Screen readers hear "Call", with "Voice call" and "Video call" as its custom actions while the menu is offered. A secret chat has search and the self-destruct timer (Material Symbols "timer", disabled until the chat is ready and once it ended; a timer that is on shows in short, "30s", on a `primary` / `onPrimary` `Badge` over the button's top end, inside its touch target; owner decision to confirm (2026-10-24): its text follows the font scale up to 130% only, as at 200% it hid the timer it labels, whose button names it in full for screen readers). Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-22): with search taking one of the two places, the encryption key ("key") moved from the top bar into the timer's sheet, its last row ("Encryption key", the key symbol), which opens the key's sheet in its place; the key is compared once, the timer and search are used more often. Calls in secret chats will need this decided again. A secret chat's name follows a lock (`ic_lock`, `sizes.inlineIcon`, `primary`, "Secret chat" for screen readers in the title's heading), and while it waits for the other user or after it ended, its state ("Waiting for Ada to come online", "Secret chat ended") replaces the status. In a private or secret chat the title (avatar, name and status, one button for screen readers, "Open profile") opens the other user's profile (`ProfileRoute`), its state layer over the title's bounds, the avatar growing into the profile with a container transform (§5; the third pane on extra-large windows, §7); in basic groups, supergroups and channels it opens their info page (`ChatInfoRoute`, "Open info" for screen readers); in Saved Messages it opens the user's own profile (`ProfileRoute`, "Open profile"), which holds Saved Messages' shared media and "Export chat". In group chats a sender's avatar and name open their profile too, on 48 dp touch targets that move nothing, and as the bubble's "Open profile" custom action; a tapped mention opens the profile it names, or the group or channel of an `@username`. While someone is doing something in the chat, typing dots precede the status (§5) and the title is a polite live region, so screen readers announce it; the status otherwise is not one, as "last seen" changes every minute. While TDLib is not connected, "Waiting for network…", "Connecting…" or "Updating…" (`bodySmall`, `onSurfaceVariant`) takes the status's place, and what someone is doing's, as Telegram's apps do, the title a polite live region meanwhile (audit A11; owner, 2026-10-23) |
| In-chat search | `REQUIREMENTS.md` §5.9. The top bar's search button turns the top bar into the search bar's `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` in its pill (`SearchBarDefaults.inputFieldShape`, the search bar's container color) on the bar's `surfaceContainer`, as tall as the top bar, at most `contentMaxWidth` wide and centred: back ("Close search") at its start, a clear button while it holds text, placeholder "Search"; it takes the focus (the keyboard opens) when the user opens search, not after a rotation or process death; the two bars cross-fade on the effects springs. The composer's place shows the search's bar, on the composer's `surface`, as tall and as wide as the composer at one line (`sizes.bubbleMaxWidth`, centred), sliding up as the composer fades out, as the selection toolbar does: "Jump to date" (`ic_calendar_month`) and, in groups, "Search by sender" (`ic_person_search`) `IconButton`s, the counter "3 of 17" ("3 of 50+" where Telegram does not count them all; "Searching…", "No results" in `onSurfaceVariant`; a "Try again" `TextButton` after a failure) in `labelLarge` with tabular figures, its own text direction, a polite live region, then "Older result" (`ic_keyboard_arrow_up`) and "Newer result" (`ic_keyboard_arrow_down`) `IconButton`s, disabled where there is none; every icon button with `IconButtonDefaults.shapes()` and a `PlainTooltip`, 48 dp targets. A picked sender replaces the sender button as a selected `InputChip` (`InputChipDefaults.shapes()`, avatar, name as user content, close symbol) that a tap removes ("Messages from Ada", "Search messages from everyone"), the counter after it. The query is searched 300 ms after the last key; the newest result shows at once and the arrows go to the others, each shown in the list as a tapped reply quote's message (loaded around it when far back, centred, the bubble flashing its jump highlight), its bubble marking the query's words: `tertiary` blocks with `small` corners behind them in `onTertiary`, drawn from the text's layout like code blocks (`textBlocks`), so no glyph moves; hidden spoilers stay hidden; screen readers hear "matches your search" in its description. The members' sheet: a `ModalBottomSheet` "Search messages from" (`titleLargeEmphasized`), the search bar's `InputField` ("Search members", searched after a pause), the members as single-choice `ListItem`s inset like the chat list's rows (40 dp avatar, name as user content, "Owner" / "Admin" in the supporting style), the picked one `large` in `secondaryContainer`; `DelayedLoadingIndicator`, "No members found", a failure with "Try again", crossfading on the effects springs with `SizeTransform` on `defaultSpatialSpec`. "Jump to date": a `DatePicker` in a `DatePickerDialog` ("Jump to date", "Cancel" / "Go"), today selected, no day after it; the list shows the first message of the day (or the next one after it, or the last before it), as a result; a day before the chat began is a snackbar. Back (predictive, the bar scaling to 90%) and the field's back close search, which forgets the query, the sender and the results; selection mode shows over it. Search sits in the top bar in every chat (Owner decision above) |
| Pinned messages bar | At the top of the conversation's sheet, inset `spacing.small` like the rows: a tonal strip (`surfaceContainerHigh`, `large` corners) with "Pinned message" (and "2 of 5", counted from the oldest as the list shows them, so the newest is "5 of 5") in `labelMedium` `primary` (two lines at most, so the count is never cut at large font scales) over one line of the message (`bodyMedium`, the sender in `onSurface`, the preview in `onSurfaceVariant`); along its start a segment per pin in the list's order, the oldest at the top and the newest at the bottom (`sizes.pinnedIndicator`, at most four, the window following the shown one), the shown one `primary`, the others `outlineVariant`, its highlight moving on `defaultSpatialSpec` in the draw phase. It shows the pinned message closest above the messages on screen (at or above the newest of them): at the bottom of the chat the newest pin, an older one as the list scrolls up past it, the oldest above every pin, as Telegram's apps do. A tap shows the message in the list and moves the bar to the next older pin (back to the newest after the oldest), until the user drags the list; the text slides vertically on `defaultSpatialSpec` (an older message from above) with effects-spring fades. Then an `IconButton` "All pinned messages" (where there are several) and close: unpin (`ic_keep_off`) for those TDLib lets unpin, hide (`ic_close`) for the others, until a newer pin, with a snackbar. It grows in and shrinks away (`expandVertically` on `defaultSpatialSpec`). Screen readers hear one item with the buttons as custom actions. The list is a `ModalBottomSheet` ("Pinned messages", `titleLargeEmphasized`), the pins oldest first as `ListItem`s inset like the chat list's rows, opening at the bar's one, which is selected (`secondaryContainer`, `large`) |
| Translate bar | Where Telegram suggests translating the chat (td_api.tl `chat.is_translatable`, Telegram Premium) and most of its recent incoming messages are in a language the user does not read, as the device detects it: at the top of the conversation's sheet, below the pinned messages bar and inset as it is (`spacing.small` from the sides and above), a tonal strip on `surfaceContainerHigh` with `large` corners: Material Symbols "translate" in `primary`, then what a tap does in `labelLarge` `primary` ("Translate to English", "Show original" once on) over the chat's language or who translates ("Chat in Spanish", "Messages translated by Telegram") in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`, one line each, ellipsized, and a close `IconButton` (`IconButtonDefaults.shapes()`). A tap turns the chat's translation on or off with `ToggleOn` / `ToggleOff` haptics, the text crossfading on the effects springs (`SizeTransform` on `defaultSpatialSpec`); close hides it for that chat on this device, with a snackbar. It grows in and shrinks away like the pinned bar (`expandVertically` on `defaultSpatialSpec`). Screen readers hear one button ("Translate to English, Chat in Spanish") with "Close translate bar" as its custom action. Language names in the screen's language |
| Scheduled messages bar | `REQUIREMENTS.md` FR-C5. Where the chat holds messages the user scheduled (td_api.tl `chat.has_scheduled_messages`): at the top of the conversation's sheet, below the pinned messages and translate bars and inset as they are (`spacing.small` from the sides and above), a tonal strip on `surfaceContainerHigh` with `large` corners: Material Symbols "schedule_send" in `primary`, "3 scheduled messages" in `labelLarge` `primary` over when the first goes out ("Next: Today, 6:30 PM", "When online") in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`, one line each, ellipsized, crossfading on the effects springs as they change (`SizeTransform` on `defaultSpatialSpec`). A tap opens the list. It grows in and shrinks away like the pinned bar (`expandVertically` on `defaultSpatialSpec`); screen readers hear one button. Only in the whole chat's conversation, not in a topic's or a thread's. Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-23): a bar below the other two, as their pattern sets, rather than a top-bar action (whose two places hold search and a call or the timer) or an entry in the composer's send menu; it costs a row of height while there are scheduled messages |
| Welcome pack | The owner's board 6 (td_api.tl `chat.has_welcome_messages`, the group's `welcomeMessage`s, which only new members see). At the top of a group's conversation, below the pinned, translate and scheduled messages bars and inset as they are (`spacing.small`), a card on `surfaceContainerHigh` with `large` corners: the megaphone symbol and "Welcome message · only you see this" in `labelMedium` `primary` (a heading), each welcome message with its photo (`sizes.avatarLarge`, `large` corners, dimmed in dark theme as the conversation's media) beside its text (`bodyMedium`, user content, its formatting kept), the messages scrolling past `sizes.mediaMaxHeight`, then "Got it" (`FilledTonalButton`, `ButtonDefaults.shapes()`) at the end. "Got it" collapses it for good on this device into one line, "Welcome message" over the first message's start in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`, a tap opening it again ("Open the welcome message"); the two crossfade on the effects springs with `SizeTransform` on `defaultSpatialSpec`, and the card grows in and shrinks away as the pinned bar. Not in channels, topics or threads; nothing asked of Telegram where the group has no welcome messages. Owner decisions to confirm (2026-11-10): the pack sits at the top of the conversation with the other bars rather than among the messages after "You joined the group", since the history may not reach the join, and stays until "Got it" for every member of a group that has welcome messages (td_api.tl says nothing of when the user joined); the admins' tags beside the messages and opening the photo full screen are left out, as welcome messages are no messages of the chat
| Scheduled messages | `ScheduledMessagesRoute`, pushed above the conversation (shared axis X, also from the Chats tab's detail pane): a small `TopAppBar` with back and "Scheduled messages" (`titleLargeEmphasized`) on `surfaceContainer`, the messages on a `ContentSheet` as the conversation's own bubbles (outgoing, `MessageBubbleRow`), laid out as the conversation is: a reverse-layout keyed and content-typed `LazyColumn`, the latest send time at the bottom, grouped by sender as there, under date pills by send day ("Today", "Tomorrow", "Friday, October 30"), those that wait for the other user last under "When Ada is online". Every bubble shows its meta: the "schedule_send" symbol, a repeating one's repeat symbol and "Daily", then its send time ("When online" in its place), no read checks; one that failed to reach Telegram its "Not sent" label. Screen readers hear "Sends 8:50 AM, repeats: Daily" as the bubble's time. A tap or a long press (or screen readers' actions) opens a `ModalBottomSheet` as the message actions sheet is: when it goes out in `titleMedium` (a heading) with the repeat in `bodyMedium` `onSurfaceVariant` beside the "schedule_send" symbol in `primary`, a divider, then "Send now", "Reschedule" (the composer's `DatePicker` + `TimePicker` in a `DatePickerDialog`, at the message's time), "Edit" where TDLib allows it, "Copy text" where the chat lets its content be saved, and "Delete" in `error`, behind an `AlertDialog`; results are snackbars (a flood wait's countdown text). Edit puts the conversation's composer in edit mode at the bottom (its bar, the round check, emoji only), nothing new is sent from here. States: `DelayedLoadingIndicator`; none (or none left) an `EmptyState` (`Cookie9Sided`, "schedule_send"), which stays rather than closing the screen; a failure `Cookie4Sided` with "Try again". Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-23): the actions in a sheet, not the conversation's floating menu over the lifted bubble, which brings the reactions and the blur with it; and the empty state shown rather than the screen closing itself once the last message went out |
| Message translation | "Translate" in a message's actions (after "Copy text", Material Symbols "translate"), for a text the device did not find in a language the user reads; "Show original" there once translated, and as the bubble's custom action. The bubble shows the translation in place of its text (`bodyLarge` as user content, its own direction), and its meta shows, before the time, the translate symbol (`sizes.inlineIconSmall`) and "Translated from Spanish" ("Translated" when the language is unknown) in `labelSmall` in the meta's color, ellipsized first where the meta has no room; a translated bubble always shows its meta. While Telegram translates, the bubble keeps its text and its meta shows a `LoadingIndicator` at the icon's size with "Translating…". Screen readers hear the label before the text. Failures are snackbars: a flood wait's countdown text, no network, Telegram Premium required, else "Telegram couldn't translate this message" |
| Message summaries | Telegram's summary of a long post (td_api.tl `summarizeMessage`, offered where the message has a `summary_language_code`: long channel posts and the like): "Summarize" in its actions, after "Translate" where both show (Material Symbols "format_list_bulleted"), "Show full text" once summarized, also the bubble's custom action; asked for only on that tap, in the reader's language where the post is in one they do not read and Telegram translates into it, else in its own. The bubble shows the summary in place of its text (`bodyLarge` as user content, its own direction), and its meta shows, before the time, the list symbol (`sizes.inlineIconSmall`) and "Summary by Telegram" in `labelSmall` in the meta's color, ellipsized first; while Telegram summarizes, a `LoadingIndicator` at the icon's size with "Summarizing…". A summarized bubble always shows its meta and offers no "Translate" (its full text is a tap away). Screen readers hear the label before the text. Failures are snackbars, as translation's: a flood wait's countdown text, no network, Telegram Premium required, else "Telegram couldn't summarize this message". Rich messages are not summarized. Owner decision to confirm (2026-11-10): the list symbol stands in for Material Symbols "summarize", which the project's vectors do not include yet; "Summary by Telegram" names who made it, as the translation label does; and only the one post is summarized, never a chat |
| Custom emoji | No component: drawn by `core:ui`'s `Modifier.customEmoji` over the text's own layout in bubbles (`MaterialTheme.chatStyle.messageText`, so they follow Chat appearance's text size), emoji-only messages (`displaySmall`) and the composer's field, and `CustomEmojiImage` in reaction badges (§4). The text keeps the regular emoji, so selection, copying and screen readers get it; the picture is decorative. No picker: Pingogram offers no custom emoji to add (sending them needs Telegram Premium in most chats) |
| Forum topics | A forum (a supergroup with topics) opens its topic list in the conversation's place, pushed or in the detail pane: the conversation's top bar (the forum's avatar, its name over "12 topics") on `surfaceContainer`, the topics on a `ContentSheet` as the chat list's rows: Material's two-line `ListItem` (72 dp), inset `spacing.small`, the topic's icon in a 48 dp circle (`sizes.avatarRow`: the General topic's `ic_tag` on `surfaceContainerHighest`, the others `ic_mode_comment_filled` in the tonal pair of the topic's color, §2.2), the name in `titleMedium` (`titleMediumEmphasized` while unread, a lock before a closed topic), the last message with its sender in `onSurface` (`bodyMedium`, one line), the time (`labelSmall`, `tnum`) over the pin, an "@" badge for mentions and the unread `Badge` in `primary`. General first, then pinned topics, then by newest message; pages load near the end, a `LoadingIndicator` or the page's error with "Try again" below the last row. States: `DelayedLoadingIndicator`, an `EmptyState` (`Cookie9Sided`), the error (`Cookie4Sided`, "Try again"). A topic opens its conversation, pushed above the list on the shared axis X (in the detail pane, in its place): the topic's name over the forum's in the top bar; a closed topic says so in the composer's place to members who are not administrators. Screen readers hear a row as one: name, closed, pinned, message, time, counts. **Managing** (`REQUIREMENTS.md` FR-C9), only what the user's rights allow (`can_manage_topics`; deleting `can_delete_messages`; a topic's creator renames, closes and reopens their own; creating the members' `can_create_topics`): "New topic" as the top bar's `FilledTonalIconButton` (`ic_add`, `IconButtonDefaults.shapes()`), as the conversation's actions; a long press on a row (the list item's `LongPress` haptic, the row in its selected colors and `large` shape while open) opens an expressive `DropdownMenuPopup` like the Contacts rows': "Edit name", "Close topic" / "Reopen topic", "Pin" / "Unpin", for General "Hide General" / "Show General" (General is never closed, pinned or deleted), outlined symbols, then "Delete topic" in `error` in a second group, behind an `AlertDialog` ("Delete topic?", that its messages go for everyone, "Delete" in `error`); the same items are the row's screen-reader custom actions. A General topic hidden from members stays in the list of those who may show it again, its name after `ic_visibility_off` ("Hidden" for screen readers). Pinned topics end in a drag handle (Pingogram's six-dot symbol, 48 dp) where they may be reordered: dragging lifts the row into the list item's dragged shape and colors, following the finger in its graphics layer while the others slide on `defaultSpatialSpec` with a `SegmentTick` each, the order shown at once and sent as the finger lifts (taken back with a snackbar if Telegram refuses); screen readers "Move up" / "Move down". In a topic's conversation the top bar's second action (after search) is "Manage topic" (`ic_edit`, a `FilledTonalIconButton`), which opens the same menu anchored to it, as the call button does; no button where the user may do nothing. The editor (new topic, or "Edit topic" for a name) is a `ModalBottomSheet` that opens expanded and scrolls: the title in `titleLargeEmphasized`; the topic's icon in the color picked (`sizes.avatarMedium`) beside the name in the rounded tonal `TextField` with "4 / 128" below it (Telegram's limit), or "The name is too long" / Telegram's refusal in `error` as a polite live region; for a new topic "Color" (`titleSmallEmphasized` `primary`) over Telegram's six colors as chips in a `FlowRow`, each a `sizes.themeChip` circle in its tonal pair (blue `primaryContainer`, green and yellow `secondaryContainer`, violet, rose and red `tertiaryContainer`) with the topic symbol, morphing into `Cookie9Sided` with a check on `primary` when picked (snapping with reduced motion), its name below it in `labelMedium` `onSurfaceVariant`, one radio group for screen readers (`SegmentTick`), and a `bodySmall` note that the colors show in the theme's own; a rename says that a topic keeps its color (`editForumTopic` changes the name only, and Pingogram offers no custom emoji icons); a `LinearWavyProgressIndicator` while Telegram answers; `TextButton` "Cancel" at the start and a default `Button` "Create" / "Save" at the end (`ButtonDefaults.shapes()`). A created topic opens; refusals and "Topic deleted" are snackbars; a topic deleted from its conversation leaves it. Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-24): three of the six colors share each tonal pair, so blue stands alone and green / yellow and violet / rose / red look alike in the chips and the rows; the chips carry the colors' names. Alternatives: Telegram's own colors harmonized toward `primary`, or the `*Fixed` roles, which §2.2 keeps for the encryption key. Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-24): "Manage topic" is an anchored menu in the top bar rather than behind the title, which opens the forum's info page |
| Saved Messages tabs | Owner decision to confirm (2026-10-25): while Saved Messages holds messages saved from more than one chat (td_api.tl `savedMessagesTopic`), its top bar is followed by tabs on its `surfaceContainer`, as the chat list's folder tabs: a connected `ButtonGroup` of `ToggleButton`s (unchecked `surfaceContainerHighest`, the selected label `labelLargeEmphasized`) in a 48 dp row inside the window margin, in a horizontal scroll that brings the selected tab into view and fades at its edges; "All" first, then the topics in Telegram's order, pinned first: "My Notes", "Author hidden" and each chat's title (user content, "Deleted chat" where Telegram no longer knows it). `Role.Tab` for screen readers. A tab fades through to its conversation on the effects springs; the top bar stays "Saved Messages". In a chat's tab the composer's place says "New messages go to My Notes" (`ComposerUiState.Unavailable`), as what is sent to Saved Messages goes there. More topics load as the row nears its end. Rather than Telegram's "view as chats" list, so Saved Messages keeps opening on its messages |
| Forum topic tabs | A forum set to show its topics as tabs (td_api.tl `supergroup.has_forum_tabs`, which the group's settings set, §6 "Group and channel settings") opens its conversation with the topics as Saved Messages' tabs (the same connected `ButtonGroup` of `ToggleButton`s in a fading horizontal scroll, `Role.Tab`) in the top bar's slot, instead of the topic list: "All" first, the whole chat, then General (left out where it is hidden from members), the pinned topics and the others by their newest message, names as user content; more topics load as the row nears its end. A tab fades through to its topic's conversation on the effects springs, as Saved Messages' do; a message found in a topic opens at that tab. The top bar stays the forum's. Owner decision to confirm (2026-11-10): "All" writes into General, as the whole chat's composer does; managing topics (§6 "Forum topics") stays in each topic's conversation and the topic list, which the tabs replace |
| Saved Messages tags | The owner's board 8 (td_api.tl `savedMessagesTag`, the reactions a Telegram Premium subscriber puts on saved messages to sort them). On Saved Messages' "All" tab, while the account has tags, a row of `FilterChip`s (`FilterChipDefaults.shapes()`) follows the topic tabs in the top bar's slot, in a horizontal scroll inside the window margin, one radio group for screen readers: "All" first (selected while nothing is filtered), then each tag's emoji (a custom emoji's tag the mood symbol, "Custom emoji" for screen readers), its label (`labelLarge`, user content) and its count (`labelMedium`). A tap filters by the tag; another tap or "All" shows everything again. The filtered view takes the conversation's place, fading through on the effects springs: a small `TopAppBar` on `surfaceContainer` with back ("Show all saved messages"), "Saved Messages" in `titleLargeEmphasized` over "3 tagged “❤ Ideas”" in `bodySmall` `onSurfaceVariant` (one heading, the count a polite live region) and a "Rename tag" `TextButton`; the chips below it; then on a `ContentSheet` the tagged messages, newest first (td_api.tl `searchSavedMessages`), as `ListItem`s inset like the chat list's rows: what each says (`bodyLarge`, two lines, user content, worded as the chat list's previews) over the day it was saved (`bodyMedium`); a tap opens it in the "All" conversation. 30 load at a time, more 5 rows from the end (a `LoadingIndicator` below them, a failure in `error` with "Try again"); the first a `DelayedLoadingIndicator`; a tag no message has any more says so. Back clears the filter. "Rename tag" (also each chip's custom action) opens a `BasicAlertDialog` in the alert dialog's shape and colors: "Rename tag" in `titleLargeEmphasized` (a heading), the label in a filled one-line `TextField` (at most 12 characters, td_api.tl's limit), a `LinearWavyProgressIndicator` while Telegram answers, `TextButton` "Cancel" and a default `Button` "Save", disabled until the label changed; renamed, a snackbar, and Telegram's refusal (Telegram Premium required) a snackbar too, never worked around. The selected tag and the dialog survive process death. Owner decisions to confirm (2026-11-10): the tagged messages show as rows of their text rather than the conversation's bubbles, so the filter needs no second conversation; the row shows on "All" only, as Telegram's tags count all of Saved Messages; a label is renamed from the filtered view's "Rename tag" (and screen readers' action) rather than a long press, which a `FilterChip` does not take; the bubbles' reaction badges stay as they are, without the board's label beside the emoji |
| Comment threads | Under a channel post of a channel with a discussion group, and under a group message others replied to in its thread: a link on the bubble's side, `spacing.extraSmall` below it (or below its reactions), an extra-small `FilledTonalButton` (`ButtonDefaults.ExtraSmallContainerHeight`, `shapesFor` press morph, `secondaryContainer` / `onSecondaryContainer`, `textStyleFor`, 48 dp touch target) with the outlined `ic_mode_comment` and "12 comments" ("Leave a comment" before the first) or "3 replies". Screen readers hear the count in the bubble's description and open it as the bubble's "Open comments" / "Open replies" custom action. It opens the thread's conversation, pushed on the shared axis X (in the detail pane, in its place): the discussion group's avatar and name over "12 comments" (`bodySmall`, what someone is doing in its place), the post first as the group has it ("Forwarded from …"), the replies after it, the composer sending into the thread |
| Polls | `REQUIREMENTS.md` FR-C4, padded in the bubble, `mediaMaxWidth` wide: the question (`titleMedium`, user content) over what kind of poll it is (`labelMedium`: "Anonymous poll", "Public quiz", "Final results" once closed). **Voting** (open, nothing forbids it, not voted yet): each answer a full-width row of at least 48 dp (`minimumInteractiveComponentSize`, `small` ripple bounds) beside an empty `RadioButton`, a tap voting at once with a `ToggleOn` haptic; where several answers are allowed, `Checkbox`es that taps check and uncheck (`ToggleOn` / `ToggleOff`) and a default `Button` "Vote" (`labelLargeEmphasized`, `shapes()`, in the bubble's accent and on-accent colors) at the footer's end, disabled until one is checked. A vote on its way to Telegram (td_api.tl `is_being_chosen`) shows a `LoadingIndicator` in place of its answer's button and takes no more taps. **Results** (voted, closed, or the poll's creator): each answer's text (`bodyMedium`, user content), its mark (`inlineIcon`: a filled check for the user's answer, in a quiz a check for the correct answer and a cancel for a wrong one) and its percentage (`labelLarge`, `tnum`), over a bar of its share (`sizes.pollBar`, round ends, the share in the bubble's accent, in a quiz `correct` green or `wrong` red, then Material's track gap and the `track` color), drawn by a `Modifier.Node` that animates a new share on `defaultSpatialSpec` in the draw phase (the first shows at once): icons and colors, never color alone. Then the voters (`labelMedium`: "100 votes", "No votes yet") and, for a public poll whose voters TDLib lists, a `TextButton` "View votes" in the accent.
